# PR quality gate

How every pull request to KratosForge gets an engineering quality decision. It sits beside the hardening release gates (`docs/hardening/readiness-checklist.md`, PR #137) and the usability gates (`docs/ux/release-review.md`, PR #166) and doesn't repeat them: those ask "is it safe" and "is it usable"; this asks "does this code deserve to exist in this form". Release management decides merges and promotes; this review recommends, and never merges or deploys.

## Scoring

Each category is scored 0 to 10 from evidence in the diff, the tests and the [standards](architecture.md#standards-the-code-follows).

| Category | Weight | A 9 looks like | A 5 looks like |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Functional correctness | 20% | Does what it says on every path, including failures; nothing reports success after a failure | Happy path works; some failure path guesses, or reports success |
| Architectural consistency | 15% | Uses the existing helper for each concern (client factory, `fsutil`, `sqlite_util`, `retrying`, `redact`, `timeutil`, `confirmDialog`, `attempt`) | Adds a new copy of something the repo already has |
| Code quality and maintainability | 15% | Small, named for what it does, no dead code, no broad silent `except` | Long functions, silent handlers, unexplained `type: ignore` |
| UX consistency and simplicity | 15% | Uses shared components, fits the navigation budget, no new configuration | Bespoke dialog or tab bar, a new setting that could be automatic |
| Testing and verification | 15% | A failing-first test for every behavior change, failure paths tested, test names match what they prove | Tests only assert what a fake was told to return |
| Security | 10% | Hardening gates hold; no new scope without `docs/permissions.md` | Unreviewed token, permission or file handling |
| Performance and efficiency | 5% | No new polling or repeated remote calls; local work stays local | New loop or repeated lookup with no limit |
| Documentation | 5% | The doc section exists, matches behavior and says what is local, compatible, simulated, remote or unsupported | Feature names repeated without behavior |

**Decision:** 9.0 and up PASS; 8.0 to 8.9 CONDITIONAL PASS (named follow-up, verified by Release management before the stated point); below 8.0 FAIL.

## Automatic failures

Any one of these is FAIL whatever the score, and can't be conditional:

1. A critical security vulnerability.
2. A known data-loss defect, including an overwrite or delete wider than the user asked for.
3. Functionality presented as working that isn't: an ignored argument, a hard-coded "measured" value, a success message after a failure.
4. A claim of test success that wasn't run.
5. A broken critical workflow.
6. A material architecture change without agreement (a new process, framework, store format or pattern for a concern the repo already handles).
7. Missing authorization control on a write.
8. A known high-severity regression.

## Investigation triggers

These start a closer look; they aren't failures on their own.

| In the diff | Check |
| --- | --- |
| `except Exception` | Does it tell "not set up" from "failed"? Does a failure reach the user? (QA-013) |
| `write_text(json.dumps(...))`, `sqlite3.connect`, `datetime.now`, `re.compile` of secret patterns | Use the shared helper (QA-011, QA-022, QA-024, QA-016) |
| `FabricClient(`, `FileStore(`, `._token(`, `requests.` | Use `Service.fabric` / `files` / `token` (QA-014) |
| A new name in `server.METHODS` | Is it called from the UI or tagged? Is `audit.READ_ONLY` right? (QA-015, QA-017) |
| A new `*_service.py` mixin | Could it be a module called by an existing mixin? |
| `showDialog` with a "…?" title, a `tab` button, a `pill-s` span, `setTimeout` loop | Use the shared UI helper (QA-019) |
| "no capacity", "identical to Fabric", "measured" | Is it verified on this execution path? (QA-008) |
| A test with `monkeypatch.setattr(svc, ...)` or a new `FakeFabric` | Does the test prove the code, or the fake? (QA-018) |
| A Fabric-writing button | Is it under a Preview tag? (QA-020) |

## Report format

One block per PR, pasted into the release record by Release management.

```
PR: #<number> <title>
Quality decision: PASS | CONDITIONAL PASS | FAIL
Score: <overall> (correctness x, architecture x, code x, UX x, tests x, security x, performance x, docs x)
Automatic failures: <none, or which and why>
Findings: <QA-id or new id, severity, file:line, one line each>
AI slop: <duplication, needless complexity, incomplete or simulated behavior, or none>
Required remediation: <what must change, or the condition for CONDITIONAL>
Tests: executed and passed | executed and failed | not executed | not verifiable without Fabric
Release decision: <merge now | merge after X | hold>
```

Every review says which tests were actually run in this review. "Static review only" when none were.

## Reviews

PR reviews start after this baseline merges and are added below, newest first.

Totals: 2 PASS, 8 CONDITIONAL PASS, 5 FAIL at first review. Follow-ups: #206 makes `previewMarked` always tag and names the always-shown steps in Settings and the user guide; #209 makes `attempt()` silent only for an explicitly cancelled sign-in, with a test that runs it on an empty error. For both, `node --test app/tests/ui.test.cjs` passed on the head (55 and 59 tests). All FAILs went to Release management through the coordinator, with the owner and fix.

What the set says about the baseline: the five FAILs are the patterns the [findings](findings.md) describe, appearing in new code. Unvalidated names building paths (QA-025), a heuristic labelled as measured (QA-008), success shown without checking (UX-014), and dry-run classification by text prefix. No PR reintroduced a silent overwrite, and every PR's new tests failed on its parent's code, which is the evidence the gate asks for.

Probe (mine): when the lock outlasts that (9 sleeps, 0.18 s), it raises PermissionError, the old file is kept unchanged and the temp file is removed, so it fails safe, never torn. Corrupt file at startup: a torn schedules.json is moved to `schedules.json.corrupt-<time>` with a warning, and the app starts with no schedules. Before, the next save silently overwrote it, so it is now recoverable. Verified: the 4 per-site tests fail with main's local_schedules.py and runtime.py. The agent's settings live under its own home (`config.home/data`), not the app's settings.json, so no non-atomic writer shares the file. Nits: (a) the retry is shorter than the Delta-log pattern (`retrying.local_retry`: 6 tries, 0.05 s doubling, about 1.5 s); a virus scanner can hold a file longer than 0.2 s, so reuse that backoff; (b) a set-aside schedules file only reaches the log, and Windows tasks already registered under FabricDesktop then point at schedules the app no longer lists; say so in Monitor > Schedules in a later slice. That closes the unsafe path: before, a 403 or a 503 read as 'not there', so `put()` without overwrite and `mv` without overwrite could replace an existing file. `mv` is now safer too: if the check after the copy fails, it raises before `rm(src)`, so the source is kept. `rm` and `cp` with recurse raise instead of acting on a guessed 'not a folder'. Verified: the 5 new raise cases fail with main's `notebookutils_local.py` (5 failed, 27 passed) and pass on the branch. Out of scope as stated: mkdirs and read.schema. Tests run: test_notebookutils_fs 32 passed; clean merge with main. |
| #306 | HD-035: Prove and side-by-side polling stop on finish, close and errors | CONDITIONAL at 1c8f67c (UI only) | 8.6 | The shared `pollRun` is sound. It backs off 1.5 s → 12 s, stops after 20 failed checks in a row, and stops at once on the engine's own messages (`LookupError("No proof …")` in prove_service.py:573, `No side-by-side run …` in side_by_side_service.py:166). A lost run is marked failed, so the buttons free up, and side by side gets the one-loop-per-run guard. Leaving the page keeps polling, because `state.notebook` is unchanged. Condition: closing the notebook (the rail's X sets `state.notebook = null`) or opening another notebook now returns early before `logActivity`. The bell entry for that proof or comparison then stays 'running' for good, and no result toast comes. The app promises the opposite: 'the bell tells you when it's done' (prove.js:129) and 'You can leave this page; the bell tells you when the comparison is ready' (sidebyside.js:198). It resumes only if the person reopens that notebook and opens Prove or the toggle. Fix: keep checking the status after the notebook closes (the backoff and the lost/20-failure stops already end the leak), and use `live` only to decide whether to redraw; or, at minimum, settle the bell entry when the follow stops. Add a test that closes the notebook mid-run and checks that the bell entry ends. Tests run: ui.test.cjs 107/107; clean merge with main. |

Fix: also match the bare path (`/webhookb2/...` and `/webhook/<id>@<id>/IncomingWebhook/...`) in both redactors, and add a shared case built from a real requests ConnectionError message. | #513 39f393d4 | Magics: a comment ending in a backslash doesn't swallow the next magic line; returns_rows reads past /* */ and quoted semicolons | PASS | 9.3 | Two bug fixes. (1) _scan now reports line continuation itself, looking only at text before a comment. "# path C:\temp\" followed by %pip, or "# c\" followed by !echo, now finds the magic, where main ran it as Python. A real continuation ("x = 1 + \"), a backslash-continued single-quoted string, an open bracket and an open triple-quoted string still keep the next line as Python. A '#' inside a string isn't a comment. (2) returns_rows uses a new _statements splitter that removes -- and /* */ comments and splits at ; only outside '...', "..." and `...`, with backslash escapes. Reviewer's probe against main: "/* c */ SELECT 1" is now True (main False); "INSERT …; SELECT 2" is True (main False, since it split only at ";" followed by a newline); "SELECT 1; INSERT … (';')" is False (main True, splitting inside the quotes); "SELECT 'a\'; DROP' AS x" and "SELECT `a;b`" are True; an unclosed /* gives False; "CREATE TABLE … AS SELECT 1;" stays False. So _sqldf, publish and depgraph see the right last statement. Both new tests fail with main's magics.py; magic, sqldf, depgraph, publish and silent_except suites 82 passed (pinned venv). Merges cleanly on main. |

| #508 98faf2f4 | Outputs hardening (stacked on #507): an index name a column already has shows as name_index; multi-level columns join with "_" | PASS | 9.0 | Closes the #507 follow-ups. pandas_plan names each index column, adding "_index" (then "_index_2", ...) while the name is taken, and joins multi-level column names with "_" (skipping empty levels). pandas_frame and Wrangler's pandas_reader apply the same plan; the generated code repr()s the names (set_axis([...]) / reset_index(names=[...])). Reviewer's probe. Index collisions can't happen: index "v" with a column "v" gives v_index, and if v_index is also taken it gives v_index_2; the Wrangler code gives the same columns. agg({"v":["sum","max"]}) shows k, v_sum, v_max. **Finding (low, follow-up): joined column names aren't de-duplicated.** Columns ("a_b","") and ("a","b") both become "a_b", so as_table raises "Duplicate column names found" and the cell ends in error. That overwrote the notebook's own temp view t: main fails `spark.sql("SELECT * FROM t").dropna()` with "infinite recursion detected" (reproduced), and two frames could read each other's rows. Each call now gets its own view, __fd_df_N, so the user's t survives and frames keep their own rows. **Blocking finding (high): the views are never dropped, and each one holds its source relation, so the data stays in memory for the rest of the session.** Reviewer's probe /mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r488_spark_views_probe.py, run twice and reproduced: dropna on 200 arrow frames of 250k rows each, then del and gc. Views went from 3 to 203 and RSS from 192 to 1011 MB; 2000 more small calls left 2203 views. The same loop on main keeps 1 view at 215 MB. A notebook calling dropna in a loop grows until the kernel runs out of memory. Dropping the view right after the query doesn't work: DuckDB re-binds the name whenever the result relation, or anything derived from it, executes, and fails with "Table with name ... does not exist". Fix: build these frames without named views (relation.filter for dropna/na.drop, aggregate and union for describe), or tie each view's lifetime to the result and everything derived from it; add a loop test asserting the view count stays bounded. (2) DDL strings work for int, bigint, string, double, decimal(10,2), date, timestamp, array<int>, map<string,int>, nested map<string,array<int>>, backticked names and the "id: int" form. "int NOT NULL", struct<...> and unknown types are refused with a clear ValueError; a trailing comma is accepted. NOT NULL and struct support are a follow-up. Unpivot nulls (owner's question): the owner is right. Spark 3.5 Dataset.unpivot builds Unpivot with no filter, so DataFrame.unpivot/melt keeps null values. Only SQL UNPIVOT drops them by default (AstBuilder adds Filter(IsNotNull) unless INCLUDE NULLS). Ours drops them (the probe's (1,'q',NULL) row is missing), and the test_spark_api.py comment at line 70 is wrong. The INCLUDE NULLS fix and the test correction can ride the fix push. The 3 new tests fail on main and pass here; test_spark_* and silent_except 105 passed (pinned venv); ui 234/234. Merges cleanly on main. |

Results map to cells by object identity (todo[i] matches out[i]), never by position in the notebook. A deleted cell's formatted text therefore couldn't land in another cell even on main. On main, setSource wrote into the detached cell object and counted it in "Formatted N cells", which also marked the notebook dirty; this PR skips that too. Moved cells keep their results. Leftover nit, not from this PR: a problem cell deleted after the toast appears leaves a stale Show cell action. A notebook that held CRLF inside its cell strings (imported, or pulled from Fabric) shows those cells as changed in a git diff once, on its next edit and save. So transforms that aren't inverses no longer recurse: (v*2, v*3) and (v+1, identity) settle after one update each way, out of step but with no error, as in traitlets. Finding (medium, regression in #451's feature): the check looks only at the cell thread's newest Python frame, and a %%sql cell, or a DataFrame shown as the cell's last line, materialises in fabric_desktop.notebook's as_table (display, _show, _run_sql), not in duckdb or fabric_desktop.spark*. So those cells lose their progress line. Sampling the cell thread during the %%sql query found it 26 of 26 times in notebook:as_table <- display <- _show <- _run_sql. | #468 9dee705 | One sign-in client at a time; get_status doesn't build one when nobody signed in (conformance flake cause) | PASS | 9.2 | (1) No deadlock: _auth_lock (a plain Lock) is held only while the Authenticator is built and is released before it is returned; every sign-in (interactive WAM, browser, silent token) runs on the returned object outside the lock, and Authenticator.__init__ never calls back into the service. A slow or stalled endpoint lookup now makes the other callers wait for the one build rather than each doing their own, and they all needed the client anyway. (2) Skipping can't hide a sign-in: get_accounts() reads MSAL's token cache, broker sign-ins included, and _save writes that cache to msal_cache.bin (default_cache_path, the same path the service's Authenticator uses) after every change; WAM accounts the app never signed in with aren't listed by get_accounts on main either. So with no client in this process and no cache file, main also answered not signed in. (3) Right after a sign-in in this engine, _auth exists, so get_status reads it; a sign-in by another process (a scheduled run) writes the cache file, which the next get_status sees. A changed client ID still rebuilds the client, now under the lock. Both new tests fail with main's two files (reviewer ran them) and pass here; integrations_conformance 39/39 three times in a row; integrations_core, auth and silent_except 65 passed. No assertion or time limit changed. Merges cleanly on main. |

| #465 14cbc50 | AI tool Python keeps the Windows processor variables (fixes a #249 regression) | PASS | 9.2 | Cause confirmed in CPython 3.13: platform._get_machine_win32 falls back to PROCESSOR_ARCHITEW6432 then PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE when its WMI query raises OSError, and _Processor.get_win32 falls back to PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER; with #249's allowlist both came back empty, and Polars' CPU check then refuses to import. Security: the four values are hardware descriptors Windows sets (architecture names like AMD64, a CPU family string, a count). They hold no secret and no path, and any process can read the same facts through WMI or GetSystemInfo, so passing them gives an approved tool nothing new; the allowlist still drops every other variable (test_the_external_environment_is_the_allowlist_and_nothing_else passes). Minimal: the architecture pair and PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER are what CPython reads; NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS is not read by CPython or any package in the pinned engine (os.cpu_count uses the system call), so it could go, though it is harmless. The new test forces the WMI failure and expects AMD64; it fails with main's kernel_proc.py (reviewer ran it) and passes here. docs/permissions.md lists the four. integrations_executions and silent_except 77 passed. Merges cleanly on main. Nit: three blank lines before the new test and one after it. |

| #258 3a05d36 | HD-022 update hand-over, re-check after merging main (#264's Arc<Sidecar>) | PASS | 9.1 | Checked the main.rs resolution with --remerge-diff: rpc is main's (lock held only to find or start the engine, ptr_eq before clearing the slot) plus the updating check under the engine lock; start_into fills Option<Arc<Sidecar>>; shut_down is reworked for the Arc. (1) Killing with holders: shut_down takes the engine out of the slot first, so no call can clone it afterwards and strong_count counts only calls in flight. A killed engine can't hand a half reply to a call: read_replies delivers a line only when reply_id parses it as whole JSON, so a truncated last line is dropped, and EOF closes Pending, so every waiting call gets Closed (SIDECAR_EXITED) and drops its Arc; a call writing to stdin gets a broken pipe and returns the same. The kill and wait paths lock child with unwrap_or_else(into_inner), and nothing panics while holding the sidecar, child, stdin or pending locks, so no mutex is poisoned. (2) Err path: hand_over calls resume(false) when stop_engine fails, which clears updating and shows the window; nothing was swapped. The slot is already empty and that engine's process was killed (an Err needs a holder, and holders trigger the kill), so the next call starts a fresh engine and the old exe keeps working. (3) Deadlocks: the lock order is sidecar then child in rpc, start_into and resume; shut_down takes each one alone and holds neither while it waits; rpc's ended branch takes only sidecar; stop_kernel takes only child; updating is an atomic, not a lock. The 10 s wait and the 90 s start wait block one async worker; rpc calls finish on spawn_blocking threads, so holders can still return. A grandchild holding the engine's stdout open would delay EOF past the grace period; that fails safe (Err, no swap), and the engine's response handle is not inherited. Rust can't be compiled here (no WebKitGTK): the CI shell job (cargo test, clippy) must be green on this head before merge, and the owner reports cargo test 56 passed. Windows file locks during the swap stay with Chris's Windows check. Merges cleanly on main. |

On the other quick-lane calls the PR names: KernelSessions.session holds an RLock, so a quick-lane create can't race the ordered lane into two sessions. poll_cell, interrupt_cell and kernel_variables never start a process (they answer idle, not interrupted or [] when none runs), but a lookup that creates a session calls _make_room, which at the cap closes the oldest idle session and clears its variables. notebook.js calls kernel_variables when a notebook opens, so opening or refreshing a notebook with no session can evict another notebook's idle session. That predates #264 (it happened in the ordered lane too) and is worth a small follow-up: create=False in those three, returning their existing empty answers. kernel_complete starts a process by design (completion needs one), so it keeps create=True. |

Reviewer's probe (/mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r441b_reaping_probe.py): a lookup during the sweep gets the same kernel, nothing is closed and nothing is left untracked, whether or not a cell starts; an idle session still closes and the next lookup makes a fresh one. The earlier r441 probe's two cases no longer happen. Nit: switch() clears _sessions but not _reaping, so after a quick process to in-engine to process switch a lookup could take back a pre-switch kernel; the sweep closes it otherwise. A caller that already held the kernel (no new lookup) and starts a cell between the running check and close is the same window as main. kernel_sessions, kernel_process, prove, silent_except tests plus the r441b and r379 probes 103 passed. | #441 ee27ff8 | Idle sweep closes a second session instead of dropping it | CONDITIONAL | 8.6 | Fixes the #435 nit for the common case: the old session with the running cell goes back in, and no process is left untracked by the sweep. Two gaps in the race it targets, both confirmed by the reviewer's probe (/mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r441_reap_second_session_probe.py). (1) The new session was handed to a caller by session()/kernel() (that lookup is what made it), and closing it strands that caller: its kernel is closed and out of the list, and ProcessKernel._ensure starts a fresh process on its next cell, which runs untracked, with variables apart from the notebook's session, and is never reaped, listed or closed by forget. (2) When both sessions have a running cell, the old one is closed mid-cell, which is worse than main, where it was only left untracked. Suggested fix: never make the second session. reap_idle keeps the sessions it takes out in a _reaping map under the lock, and session() moves a reaping session back (touched) instead of creating a new one; reap_idle then closes only what is still out and not running. kernel_sessions, kernel_process, prove, silent_except tests plus the probe 99 passed. Merges cleanly with main 535dea3. |

| #326 | OneLake write lease: docs, holder host, TTL 60 s / wait 90 s | PASS holds at 247ba2c | 9.2 | Docs now agree (agent.md Limits, notebooks.md write_table row naming the lease file and the 90 s wait, teams.md). LeaseBusy names owner and host. **TTL probe:** a write is renewed while it runs. `Held` replaces the lease every ttl/3 (20 s) on its own thread for the whole block. Probe repros/r326b_long_write.py, with TTL 0.6 s and a write lasting 3 s (5x TTL): an intruder trying every 0.2 s never got the lease, and the write committed. With every renewal failing past the TTL, the write ends with LeaseLost (reported, not silent). Metadata runs still pass ttl=seconds (120 s or `table_lease_seconds`), so only notebook and job writes use 60 s. Residual margins, worth a follow-up: each OneLake renew is up to 3 requests with `timeout=60`, so one hung renew can outlast a 60 s TTL and let another writer take over before it is marked lost (it is then reported as LeaseLost after the write); a renew timeout of ttl/3 or less would close that. Expiry compares the writer's clock against the reader's, so tolerance for clock skew between PCs drops to about 40 s. Tests run: probe 2 + test_table_leases, test_leases_local, test_agent 39 passed. Clean merge with main. |

| #322 | HD-037: Stop ends every process below the kernel, including ones in their own session or group | PASS at c8fc178 | 9.2 | `_signal_tree` SIGSTOPs the kernel, then every descendant found by ppid (/proc, or ps on macOS), rescanning up to 10 rounds until nothing new appears; then SIGKILLs each one and the group. Probes. Fork during the scan: a stopped process can't fork, and each round stops what it finds. PID reuse: a stopped process can't exit and its stopped parent can't reap it, so found pids stay theirs until the kill. The residual window is a pid that exits between being listed and being stopped, which is negligible. Only processes with a ppid chain to the kernel are signalled. The root is signalled only when `stop()` or `_end` saw `proc.poll() is None` under the lock, as before. Not covered (as before): a process that daemonized by double fork before Stop (reparented to init), and children of a kernel that had already crashed. Evidence: test_kernel_process 15 passed in 3 of 3 runs; with main's kernel_proc.py, 2 failed and left exactly 4 `sleep(120)` processes running (3 session leaders + 1 group member), which I killed; the head's runs left none. Tests run: validate + prove + notebook tests 170 passed. Clean merge with main. |

Every new file is reserved with `open(…, 'xb')`, so a name race stops instead of overwriting. Delete writes the copy and checks it before `unlink`. An interruption leaves the notebook in place (at worst also in Recently deleted, or an orphan trash file). Schedules are turned back on if the delete raises. Restore uses `xb` and leaves renamed restores' schedules off. A non-404 check error stops the publish. The docs are honest that only cells are compared and that first publishes aren't checked. Defect: `_fabric_change` reads the LINKED item (`link['item_id']`), but `save_notebook` writes the item whose displayName is the notebook's name. If the linked item was renamed in Fabric and someone created a new item with the old name, the check reports unchanged and Publish replaces that new item's cells without any warning. Repro `quality/repros/r320_renamed_item_test.py`: check `changed: False` on item-1, then item-2's cells become `['a = 10']`. Fix: resolve the item Publish will write (by name, as `save_notebook` does) before checking. If its id isn't the linked one, treat it as changed or unknown and require the Replace confirm (or write by the linked id). Add that test. | #316 | SCD1 and SCD2 wizard templates; `table_exists` in Fabric's lakehouse module | PASS at 6b924ce (draft; writes only the dimension the user names, as an overwrite the template states) | 9.2 | SCD2 logic, probed through a real Delta round trip (`quality/repros/r316_scd2_edges.py`). Unchanged rows stay current and keep their `valid_from`. A changed tracked column closes the row (`valid_to` = the new version's `valid_from`, the same run time) and adds version+1. New keys get version 1. A key that leaves the source stays current; if it comes back changed, it gets version 2. A NaN in a tracked float column doesn't make a new version on every run. One current row per key holds, with Int64 version and Datetime(us, UTC) types after the round trip. Null and duplicate source keys are stopped by the check cell. Late-arriving rows with their own effective dates aren't supported: validity is the run's UTC time, as the docs say, and the proof contract (keys + `version`, ignoring `valid_from` and `valid_to`) matches that. The Fabric shim's `table_exists` treats only not-found as no, so any other error is raised, never turned into an overwrite. Nit (docs, not blocking): with tracked columns named, a change to an untracked column alone is not written at all; the current row keeps the old value (phone 1 → 999 stays 1). Say so in the template text and in notebooks.md, or update untracked columns in place. The old test's job thread marked etl-01 lost and polled etl-02; when the publish landed between those two (slow runner), etl-01 looked live, so etl-02 rightly didn't take over and etl-01 ran it: an agent that is still publishing status *is* live, and `_guarded`'s `taken_over` check (agent.py:361-364) only cancels when another agent actually took the request. Reproduced the exact Windows failure (`'succeeded' == 'cancelled'`) on main's test by delaying `publish_status` 0.3 s and waiting 0.6 s between `lost` and `poll`; the same probes pass on #281's test. The new test runs `step()` then sets an event the job waits on, so `lost` always follows the publish; assertions unchanged, nothing skipped. Run: the test 5/5 in a row; test_agent_takeover.py 16/16. |

| #270 | Kernel: a poll or interrupt never starts a kernel process (fixes red main on Windows) | PASS at 4b0c632 | 9.3 | Root cause confirmed as a real product race, not a flaky test: when the limits watcher ends the process, a poll that checked `proc.poll()` just before it died reached `_request`, whose `_ensure` spawned a fresh process that answered "idle". That explains both Windows failures on main fc3bd71: the memory test got `idle`, and the interrupt test got `_limit_note`'s "Variables from earlier cells are kept" on that idle answer (execution_count 0, seconds 0). Fix: `_request(spawn=False)` for poll and both interrupts; a dead or missing process raises KernelGone, which poll already turns into `_stuck` or the ended cell; a cell start still spawns. The new test fails on main's kernel_proc.py with the same `'idle' == 'error'` and passes with the fix. Run: test_kernel_process.py 13/13 three runs in a row; full sidecar 2103 passed, 3 skipped. Main 7d3b82c's own Windows run passed (the race is intermittent), so #270's Windows CI is the confirmation. #268 closed. |

The UI autosaves 1.5 s after an edit (app.js:2086). With Publish queued behind a long ordered call (meta_run, sign-in, git), a later autosave now runs first, then the publish writes its older cells over it and sets `nb.dirty = false` (app.js:2146), so closing doesn't save again and the edits are lost. Repro /mnt/project-files/quality/repros/r264_lanes.py step 2: c242901 leaves 'OLD'; base d74daf7 (one lane) keeps 'NEW (typed after Publish)'. Also: `NotebookStore.save`/`set_extra` (notebook.py:887-939) share one fixed `<name>.ipynb.tmp`, so two lanes saving one notebook gave 160 FileNotFoundError failed saves in step 3 (0 corrupt files). Fix: keep notebook writers in the ordered lane, or serialize per notebook (per-name lock + unique tmp) and make an older publish refuse to save over a newer save. Inferred, not tested: a quick call passing QUICK_SECONDS (60 s) now kills the engine while an ordered write is in flight. **Verified good:** ids from `AtomicU64::fetch_add`; `register` before write; timeout or failed send calls `forget`, late replies dropped (replies.rs `deliver`), main.rs re-checks the id; output close wakes every waiter; engine reset only on the same `Arc` (`ptr_eq`); Python writes each reply line under one lock (step 1: 2000/2000 replies over real pipes matched, 0 broken lines); ordered lane is a single FIFO worker; `handle()` audits both lanes; QUICK identical in Rust and Python with a sync test. Run: replies.rs standalone 7/7; `rustc --test watchdog.rs` 5/5; test_server_lanes.py 5/5; full sidecar 2050 passed, 2 skipped; UI 93/93. Not run: full `cargo test` (no GTK). |

POSIX crash nit declined by the owner, accepted: once a crashed kernel's group has no members left, its id can be reused, so a later `killpg` could hit an unrelated group. The Windows Job Object path is only exercised by windows-latest CI, which was still queued when reviewed; no verdict until it has run. Linux verified: `test_stop_ends_the_processes_a_cell_started_too` and the kernel suites 12/12. Code: POSIX `start_new_session` + `killpg(SIGKILL)`; Windows job with KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, typed ctypes handles, falls back to killing the kernel only if the job can't be made; every former `proc.kill()` (stop, limits, failed start) now ends the tree. Nit: on Linux/macOS a kernel that crashes leaves the processes its cells started running, because `release()` ("processes a crashed kernel left behind") only acts on Windows; `killpg` on the old group would cover it |
